- **xin-ideas**: 灵感收集与多维度体检 + 可落地拆解工具。当用户抛出新想法/灵感、希望先记录并评估（而非立即实现）时使用：对想法做十维评价（解决的问题/目标用户/实用性/市场价值/竞品分析/变现可能/技术方案/可扩展性/实现计划/实现可行性）并 1-10 打分。其中「解决的问题」须明确是真实痛点还是想象问题；「市场价值」须论述市场运营与推广的可行性、难度与代价；「实现可行性」要求客观列出落地所需的技术能力与配套资源（硬件/数据/资质/渠道/人力），并明确成本与代价依据；同时给出好处/坏处、可能遇到的问题与应对、分阶段落地路线图、要避免的坑、成功失败判据与成本投入，生成精美 HTML 详情页（.xin-ideas/ideas/idea_&lt;时间戳&gt;.html）与卡片列表页（.xin-ideas/index.html，支持搜索、排序、点击跳转）。**默认对每个灵感都做完整十维打分**，不做区分；只有用户明确说「不要打分」「先记着」「只记一下」时才走轻量记录（不打分）。触发词与场景：「收集灵感」「记个想法」「灵感库」「xin-ideas」「记一笔」「做个灵感评估」「把想法存一下」「给这个想法打个分」。本 skill 只做评估与归档，不实现想法。 ([oscar-wang-xin/xin-ideas](https://github.com/oscar-wang-xin/xin-ideas/tree/HEAD/xin-ideas) · ★1)
- **xin-ji-li**: 何时调用：当用户过度依赖外部权威/理论/标准来指导决策，或感到"道理都懂但做不到"时。 关键信号："我应该听谁的？""书上说要这样但我做不到""外面的世界太复杂了我不知道怎么选"。 何时不调用：用户需要的是外部知识学习（用 lun-yu 的 xue-si-bian-zheng），或用户需要的是具体决策方法（用 mao-xuan 的 maodun-fenxi）。 ([jianjian12138/cangjie-classics-skills](https://github.com/jianjian12138/cangjie-classics-skills/tree/HEAD/yang-ming-xin-xue/xin-ji-li))
- **xin-ji-li-shi-shuo**: 何时调用：当用户对"心即理"有理论困惑，需要通过具体案例来理解"理在心中"时。 关键信号："心即理怎么理解？"、"理真的在心中吗？"、"我怎么相信自己的判断？"。 何时不调用：用户需要的是理论论证（用 yang-ming-xin-xue 的 xin-ji-li），或用户需要的是逻辑分析（用 mao-xuan）。 ([jianjian12138/cangjie-classics-skills](https://github.com/jianjian12138/cangjie-classics-skills/tree/HEAD/chuan-xi-lu/xin-ji-li-shi-shuo))

- **xingtu-article-archive**: 把公众号已发布文章归档同步到 GitHub 公开仓（xingtu-articles）+ 个人站（xingtu-site）并维护封面。当用户说"归档已发布文章""同步文章到 GitHub""补文章镜像""更新个人站文章列表""维护封面""其他文章排版/配图/素材没做好"时使用。覆盖：从发布档案台账核对已发布清单 → 比对 xingtu-articles 缺口 → 取本地成稿镜像（frontmatter/配图/结尾模块）→ 脱敏 → privacy_scan → 个人站 ARTICLES 同步 → SSH 推送 → CHANGELOG。 ([xingtu1996/xingtu-skills](https://github.com/xingtu1996/xingtu-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/xingtu-article-archive))

- **xingtu-blue-ocean-strategy**: 蓝海市场与受众战略分析（行途 XingTu）——当用户要求为自己/公司/产品/内容账号/app/创业项目做市场调研、蓝海判断、受众与变现人群分析、需求缺口识别、定位与主方向锁定、90 天战略路线图时使用。也适用于「帮我分析下这个方向/赛道/人群」「这个市场还有没有机会」「内容/产品该往哪走」类问题。六步流程：市场环境 → 人群量化漏斗 → 受众分层 → 缺口选题 → 方向决策 → 数据可信度。触发词：蓝海分析、市场调研、受众分析、变现人群、人群量化、方向锁定、定位分析、战略分析、内容缺口、赛道分析。 ([xingtu1996/xingtu-skills](https://github.com/xingtu1996/xingtu-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/xingtu-blue-ocean-strategy))
- **xingtu-collab-evolution-loop**: 行途 XingTu 通用方法论——AI 协作偏好自我进化闭环。当用户要做"复盘/沉淀/调研/方法论/把协作偏好变成系统/让 AI 少犯错"类任务时使用。自动加载四环工作流（调研取证→复盘归因→沉淀SSoT→反哺约束）+ 四不防错自检，让 AI 从"靠记忆归纳"升级为"多源取证、带出处归因、SSoT 沉淀、自动约束反哺"，减少反复踩坑。适用任何"人带 AI 长期协作"的场景，不限于内容创作。 ([xingtu1996/xingtu-skills](https://github.com/xingtu1996/xingtu-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/xingtu-collab-evolution-loop))
- **xingtu-content-craft**: 行途 XingTu 成稿总装技能：从选题素材到可排版正文 v1 的全流程。串联选题素材 → 正文 v1 起草（骨架对齐 B15/B16 基准）→ de-ai-flavor 去 AI 味 → 脱敏三查（SAF-004/005/010）→ PUB-027 量化门禁（加粗≤8/引用≤3/AI 腔 0/emoji 0）→ 信源独立成节（## N. 工具与信源）。当用户说"写稿/改稿/成稿/起草这篇/正文 v1/开始写"时触发。与 publish-pack-planning（规划层）/ mp-publish-sop（执行层：成稿→草稿箱）/ de-ai-flavor（词级 AI 味检测）/ writing-guidelines（单篇文风审查）互补：本技能管「从素材到一篇可交付排版的 MD 正文」的总装顺序，不复制各下游技能的实现。 ([xingtu1996/xingtu-skills](https://github.com/xingtu1996/xingtu-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/xingtu-content-craft))
- **xingtu-expression-logic**: 行途 XingTu 内容表达逻辑（万粉博主结构 × 不装原则）。写标题、开头、选题、结构时自检： 用户视角（你想听的，不是我想说的）/ 信息密度（每句推进信息）/ 开头两秒四公式 （痛点前置·身份反差·利益承诺·反常识）/ 植入个人故事建立对人的期待感。 硬约束：不装——禁表演词（收编/喂饱/碾压/炸裂/强制/没人当回事），行内人一眼假的词一律毙。 触发词：表达逻辑、爆款结构、怎么起标题、开头怎么写、标题太平、像营销号、太装、 不装、你视角、万粉、开头两秒、信息密度、expression logic。 ([xingtu1996/xingtu-skills](https://github.com/xingtu1996/xingtu-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/xingtu-expression-logic))
- **xingtu-ingest**: 素材入库总控（行途 XingTu）——当用户说"入库/收入素材库/存到素材库/记一下这条素材/把这个收进选题池/分析并入库"并给出内容（文本/链接/对话/想法/截图描述）时使用。自动分析内容价值分级（黄金/关键/重点/高知），按类型落到素材卡/选题池/黄金会话萃取/时间线，登记索引。触发词：入库、存素材、收素材、加入素材库、记素材、收进选题池、分析入库。 ([xingtu1996/xingtu-skills](https://github.com/xingtu1996/xingtu-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/xingtu-ingest))
- **xingtu-knowledge-compounding**: 内容创作「价值导向 + 知识资产复利」方法论总装（姜胡说三次认知升维版，🆕 10-15 boss 提炼沉淀）： 成稿/出片前的价值导向自检（拍前三问）→ 内容结构公式（产品四要素）→ 拍完/写完后的资产化（知识卡片提取指令 + 审核四问 + §八 入库五步 SOP → 叠加检索）。 触发：写/拍内容前自检「这篇对谁有用」、想把内容沉淀成知识资产、知识卡片提取、内容复利方法论、姜胡说结构、信息vs知识落地。 与 mp-publish-sop Step 1b（成稿七问）/ 专题 spec 202610142325（知识/信息/信息组织）/ METHOD-005（知识复利）互补：本技能管「内容→知识资产的通用方法论」，不复制各下游实现。 ([xingtu1996/xingtu-skills](https://github.com/xingtu1996/xingtu-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/xingtu-knowledge-compounding))
- **xingtu-multiplatform-distribution**: 公众号首发后的多平台分发流水线——按平台规格生成变体（掘金/知乎/CSDN/小红书/抖音/X）+ 变体过 de_ai_flavor_check 去AI味 + 配图（HTML→PNG 卡片；母稿非表格信息图按本地绝对路径内嵌进变体 MD 并出富文本内嵌图 HTML，表格在掘金/CSDN/知乎保留原生 markdown）+ 逐平台发布操作单 + 排产节奏（一天≤2平台）+ 发布后 evidence_register 一条命令登记凭证重算闭环率，落盘 `outputs/发布包_YYYY-MM/多平台分发_&lt;母稿&gt;_&lt;日期&gt;/`。当用户说"铺多平台/分发这篇/掘金知乎版准备一下/图卡/变体/这篇往其他平台发/分发排期"时触发。专治"稿发了、其他渠道为零"与"通稿复制粘贴被限流"（PUB-004）。只生成物料与操作单，发布动作由 boss 执行。 ([xingtu1996/xingtu-skills](https://github.com/xingtu1996/xingtu-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/xingtu-multiplatform-distribution))
- **xingtu-orchestrate**: 行途 XingTu 动态编排入口（ZCode 版 ultracode 语义 + BDD 自然语言驱动）——boss 说一句人话目标， 主会话自动解析意图 → 匹配编排模式 → 派发子代理 → 后台并行执行 → 逐站过闸收口。 行为规格用 Given-When-Then 场景写：人可读、AI 可执行、场景即验收标准。 路径动态、闸门静态：编排路径运行时现定，发布/隐私/去AI味门禁一律不免检； 自动到 S5 门禁为止，S6 发布动作受 D1 条件化授权约束（待 boss 拍板）。 编排骨架 SSoT 在 workflows/06_多任务并行编排.md，本 skill 是它的 ZCode 动态执行入口， 不复制其正文。触发词：编排跑、组队干、流水线跑、并行编排、动态编排、ultracode， 或直接说自然语言目标（「把这篇分发到掘金知乎」「这篇走完整条流水线」）。 ([xingtu1996/xingtu-skills](https://github.com/xingtu1996/xingtu-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/xingtu-orchestrate))
- **xingtu-oss-guard**: 行途 XingTu 开源矩阵的一体式安全与卫生巡检 + 历史清洗。四条检查面（工作区隐私 / git 提交历史 / 远程状态 / 外部部署面）串成一条命令，解决「各工具各报各的绿、合起来仍漏」的问题。触发词：开源巡检、oss guard、矩阵体检、历史清污、身份泄漏检查、force-push 前检查、发布前安全扫描、仓库卫生。 ([xingtu1996/xingtu-skills](https://github.com/xingtu1996/xingtu-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/xingtu-oss-guard))
- **xingtu-role-audit**: AI 头衔定位自查（三数审计）——把自治度、护栏密度、边际收益三个数量出来，输出可视化 HTML 报告 + 前进方向 + 头衔落位（Vibe/AI Native/Harness/Agentic/Builder/FDE）。触发词：自查三数、角色定位、AI 头衔定位、自治度护栏、role audit、我是哪种工程师。 ([xingtu1996/xingtu-skills](https://github.com/xingtu1996/xingtu-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/xingtu-role-audit))
- **xingtu-rule-keeper**: 行途 XingTu 规则管家——规则体系的「管理者」（不是又一份规则副本）。当需要①查询某项任务要守哪些规则（写稿/选题/封面/发布/复盘/数据/品牌/变现）②给规则体系做一致性体检（计数漂移、ID 孤儿悬空、档期事实源指针、冻结口径、手册状态、skill 违规复制正文）③新增/修改一条编号规则并要求分册/索引/计数/CHANGELOG 一次改齐时使用。触发词：规则体检、规则lint、规则查询、我要守哪些规则、加规则、新增规则、规则漂移、规则一致性、rule-keeper。注意：规则正文 SSoT 永远在 rules/RULES.d，本 skill 只实时抽取、检查、编排，绝不复制正文。 ([xingtu1996/xingtu-skills](https://github.com/xingtu1996/xingtu-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/xingtu-rule-keeper))

([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/scripting/xmake-color-output) · ★24)
- **xmake-commands**: Use when invoking Xmake from the command line — configuring, building, running, cleaning, installing, packing, or inspecting a project. Covers the common flags for `xmake f`, `xmake`, `xmake run`, `xmake install`, `xmake pack`, and friends. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/cli/xmake-commands) · ★24)
- **xmake-cross-compilation**: Use when the user is cross-compiling a C/C++ project with xmake — targeting Android/iOS/embedded/MinGW/WASM, pointing at an SDK/NDK root, overriding cc/cxx/ld/ar, selecting a built-in cross toolchain, or defining a custom toolchain in xmake.lua. Covers the full `xmake f` flag set and common SDK layouts. For generic toolchain switching on the *host* platform (e.g. gcc ↔ clang), `xmake-toolchains` is enough. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/toolchains/xmake-cross-compilation) · ★24)
- **xmake-csharp**: Use when building C# projects with xmake — `.cs` sources with the Mono `mcs` or .NET `dotnet` toolchain, binary / library output, adding references, and mixing with native code via P/Invoke. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/languages/xmake-csharp) · ★24)
- **xmake-cuda**: Use when building CUDA projects with xmake — `.cu` sources, `--cuda=` / `--cuda_sdkver=` SDK pinning, GPU architecture selection, device linking (`build.cuda.devlink`), and mixing CUDA with C++ targets. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/languages/xmake-cuda) · ★24)
- **xmake-custom-plugins**: Use when authoring a custom xmake plugin / task — defining `task(...)` with `set_menu`, running logic in `on_run`, packaging as a standalone plugin under `~/.xmake/plugins/` or inside an xmake-repo, using `import()` to reuse xmake core modules, and distributing plugins. Complements the lighter `xmake-plugins` skill which covers *using* plugins. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/scripting/xmake-custom-plugins) · ★24)
- **xmake-cxx-modules**: Use when building C++20 modules with xmake — setting up `.mpp`/`.cppm`/`.ixx` sources, enabling `build.c++.modules`, configuring per-compiler quirks (clang/gcc/msvc), `moduleonly` libraries, and distributing module packages. Covers typical project layouts for executable/library/headeronly/moduleonly targets. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/toolchains/xmake-cxx-modules) · ★24)
- **xmake-debug-package-source**: Use when a package from `add_requires` / xmake-repo fails to build or misbehaves, and you need to debug its source — editing the upstream files in place without patches, running the recipe against a local source tree with `-d &lt;dir&gt;`, stepping through `on_install`, extracting the patch once fixed, and remote debugging via `--remote`. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/packages/xmake-debug-package-source) · ★24)
- **xmake-dev**: Use when hacking on Xmake itself — building the `xmake` binary from source, loading the local source tree so your changes take effect without reinstalling, and debugging either the Lua scripts under `xmake/` or the native C core under `core/src/xmake`. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/ops/xmake-dev) · ★24)
- **xmake-distributed-compilation**: Use when setting up or using xmake's built-in distributed compilation (distcc-equivalent) to offload compile jobs to a cluster of servers. Covers `xmake service --distcc`, server/client config, token auth, job control, combining with local/remote cache, and distributed cross-compilation (Android/iOS). ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/performance/xmake-distributed-compilation) · ★24)
- **xmake-dlang**: Use when building D projects with xmake — binary/library targets with `.d` sources, DUB package integration via `add_requires("dub::...")`, and selecting DMD / LDC / GDC compilers. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/languages/xmake-dlang) · ★24)
- **xmake-env-vars**: Use when configuring xmake via environment variables — `XMAKE_PROGRAM_DIR`/`XMAKE_PROGRAM_FILE` (local source debug), `XMAKE_GLOBALDIR`/`XMAKE_CONFIGDIR` (config locations), `XMAKE_COLORTERM`/`XMAKE_PROFILE`, `XMAKE_RCFILES` (global defaults), `XMAKE_ROOT`, and how to list/inspect them with `xmake show -l envs`. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/ops/xmake-env-vars) · ★24)
- **xmake-feature-check**: Use when probing the host/toolchain for capabilities inside `xmake.lua` — `has_cfuncs` / `has_cxxtypes` / `has_features`, `check_csnippets`, `check_cflags` / `check_cxxflags`, option auto-probes (`add_cxxincludes` / `add_cfuncs`), and `lib.detect.find_*`. Covers how to gate target config on whether a symbol/header/flag is actually usable on the target platform. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/project-config/xmake-feature-check) · ★24)
- **xmake-fortran**: Use when building Fortran projects with xmake — binary/library targets with `.f`/`.f90`/`.for` sources, gfortran or Intel Fortran (`ifort`) toolchain selection, and mixing Fortran with C interoperability. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/languages/xmake-fortran) · ★24)
- **xmake-go**: Use when building Go projects with xmake — binary/library targets with `.go` sources, go module integration, cross-compiling Go programs for other OS/arch, and mixing Go with C via cgo. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/languages/xmake-go) · ★24)
- **xmake-graph-module**: Use when writing xmake scripts that need a generic graph / DAG data structure — `core.base.graph` for directed or undirected graphs, adding vertices/edges, topological sort, cycle detection, cloning, reversing. Lower-level than `async.jobgraph` — use this when you need the graph but not the scheduler. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/scripting/xmake-graph-module) · ★24)
- **xmake-harness**: Use when running or configuring `xmake ai` — the terminal coding agent provided by the xmake-harness addon. Covers installing it, setting an llm provider and api key, sessions (`-c`/`-r`), permission modes, the non-interactive `--print` mode for scripts and CI, and listing its skills/agents/tools/commands. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/ai/xmake-harness) · ★24)
- **xmake-introspection**: Use when you need to inspect an Xmake project programmatically instead of guessing — listing targets, reading a target's real sourcefiles/deps/packages/flags, exporting the target dependency graph as JSON or DOT, dumping the package dependency graph with `xrepo info --depgraph`, or querying any project data from a Lua script. Prefer this over reading xmake.lua by hand. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/cli/xmake-introspection) · ★24)
- **xmake-kotlin**: Use when building Kotlin/Native projects with xmake — `.kt` sources for native (not JVM) targets, using the `kotlin-native` toolchain, producing executables and static/shared libraries. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/languages/xmake-kotlin) · ★24)
- **xmake-link-order**: Use when fixing linker errors caused by symbol-order or circular-dependency issues in static libraries — `add_linkorders`, `add_linkgroups` (GNU ld `--start-group`/`--end-group`), whole-archive (`-Wl,--whole-archive`), weak linking, and library strip-off problems. Covers macOS, Linux, MSVC specifics. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/project-config/xmake-link-order) · ★24)
- **xmake-network**: Use when package downloads are slow, failing, or blocked — especially in China or behind corporate networks. Covers HTTP/SOCKS5 proxies (`xmake g --proxy`), host allow-lists, `pac.lua` routing, `mirror()` URL rewriting for GitHub acceleration, manual download directories, and recommended mirror setups for domestic network environments. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/packages/xmake-network) · ★24)
- **xmake-nim**: Use when building Nim projects with xmake — binary/library targets with `.nim` sources, choosing the C backend (the default) vs C++, and mixing Nim with C. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/languages/xmake-nim) · ★24)
- **xmake-objc**: Use when building Objective-C or Objective-C++ projects with xmake — `.m` / `.mm` sources, Cocoa / Foundation frameworks via `add_frameworks`, mixing with C++, and targeting macOS / iOS / tvOS. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/languages/xmake-objc) · ★24)
- **xmake-options**: Use when defining user-configurable build options (`option(...)`), reading them from targets, or exposing them on the `xmake f` command line. Covers showmenu, default, values, and option-driven config.h generation. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/project-config/xmake-options) · ★24)
- **xmake-packages**: Use when adding third-party C/C++ dependencies to an Xmake project — via `add_requires` / `add_packages`, configuring package options, pinning versions, or using `xrepo` from the command line. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/project-config/xmake-packages) · ★24)
- **xmake-pascal**: Use when building Pascal projects with xmake — `.pas`/`.pp`/`.lpr` sources, FreePascal (`fpc`) / Delphi toolchain, binary and shared-library targets. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/languages/xmake-pascal) · ★24)
- **xmake-plugins**: Use when invoking built-in Xmake plugins (project generators, compile_commands, doxygen, etc.) or writing a custom task/plugin with `task(...)`. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/scripting/xmake-plugins) · ★24)
- **xmake-policy**: Use when setting build policies via `set_policy("name", value)` — named feature toggles that control sanitizers, LTO, warnings, C++ modules, ccache, module-reuse, rpath, preprocessor behavior, package download strict mode, and many others. Covers the common policies grouped by purpose, how to scope them (project vs target), and where to find the full list. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/project-config/xmake-policy) · ★24)
- **xmake-private-packages**: Use when distributing C/C++ libraries *inside a company or team* — packaging a library with `xmake package`, creating a private package repository (local directory or git), writing package recipes for source/binary distribution, and consuming them via `add_repositories` + `add_requires`. For shipping end-user installers (zip/nsis/deb), see `xmake-xpack`. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/packages/xmake-private-packages) · ★24)
- **xmake-project-generator**: Use when exporting an xmake project to another build system or IDE — `compile_commands.json` for clangd/LSP, `CMakeLists.txt`, `build.ninja`, Visual Studio solution (`vsxmake`), or an Xcode project. Covers `xmake project -k &lt;kind&gt;` and how each generator is typically used. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/cli/xmake-project-generator) · ★24)
- **xmake-remote-compilation**: Use when compiling and running a project on a remote machine transparently — edit locally, build/run/debug on a Linux/macOS/Windows server. Covers `xmake service` (remote build server), multi-host client config, token/password auth, `--sync`/`--pull`, and the difference from distributed compilation. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/performance/xmake-remote-compilation) · ★24)
- **xmake-repo-testing**: Use when adding or modifying a package recipe in the xmake-repo repository (github.com/xmake-io/xmake-repo) and you need to test-build it locally. Wraps the `xmake l scripts/test.lua` workflow with `--shallow`, platform/arch/mode/kind/configs flags so package changes can be verified quickly before submitting a PR. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/packages/xmake-repo-testing) · ★24)
- **xmake-rules**: Use when applying built-in rules (e.g. `mode.debug`, `mode.release`, `qt.widgetapp`, `c++.unity_build`) or writing a custom rule to handle a new file extension or codegen step. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/project-config/xmake-rules) · ★24)
- **xmake-rust**: Use when building Rust projects with xmake — binary/library targets with `.rs` sources, Cargo package integration via `add_requires("cargo::...")` or full `Cargo.toml` management, C↔Rust interop with `cxxbridge`, `rustc` edition / toolchain selection, and cross-compile. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/languages/xmake-rust) · ★24)
- **xmake-script-modules**: Use when writing custom logic in `xmake.lua` and you need the day-to-day script-domain APIs — `os.*` (exec/cp/mv/ls/getenv), `io.*` (readfile/writefile/lines), `path.*` (join/basename/translate), `table.*` (xmake-extended), `string.*`, `hash.*`, `print`/`cprint`/`vprint`, `try/catch`, `raise`. Reference of what's always available without `import()`. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/scripting/xmake-script-modules) · ★24)
- **xmake-scripting**: Use when writing custom Lua script logic inside `xmake.lua` — `on_load`/`on_build`/`after_build` hooks, `import()`, extracting script files under a `modules/` dir, importing xmake core modules (`core.base.option`, `lib.detect.find_tool`, …), or building native C/C++ Lua modules via `add_rules("module.shared" / "module.binary")` with `add_moduledirs`. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/scripting/xmake-scripting) · ★24)
- **xmake-skills**: Use when the task involves Xmake — the cross-platform build utility based on Lua. This is the navigation guide for the xmake-skills bundle: it lists the focused per-topic skills and tells you which to load for which Xmake task. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/.dsh-plugin) · ★24)
- **xmake-style**: Use when writing or reviewing an `xmake.lua` (project, package recipe, or toolchain) and you want to follow idiomatic xmake style — description vs script domain separation, naming, indentation, `set_` vs `add_`, option/config organization, and common conventions used across xmake-repo and the xmake project itself. Apply this alongside `xmake-targets`/`xmake-packages`/etc. as a stylistic overlay. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/basics/xmake-style) · ★24)
- **xmake-swift**: Use when building Swift projects with xmake — binary/library targets with `.swift` sources, Swift ↔ C++/Objective-C interop (`swift.interop` value), Swift module name configuration, and iOS/macOS builds. - **xmake-targets**: Use when configuring targets in xmake.lua — setting kind (binary/static/shared), adding files/includes/links, defining dependencies between targets, and applying per-target compile/link flags. - **xmake-templates**: Use when creating a new xmake project from a built-in or third-party template (`xmake create -t &lt;template&gt;`), listing available templates, or authoring and distributing custom templates (built-in, user-global, or via xmake-repo). Covers template layout, `${TARGET_NAME}` / `${FAQ}` substitution, and language selection. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/basics/xmake-templates) · ★24)
- **xmake-tests**: Use when setting up or running tests in an Xmake project — declaring test cases with `add_tests`, running them via `xmake test`, and wiring unit-test frameworks like gtest/catch2/doctest. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/testing/xmake-tests) · ★24)
- **xmake-theme**: Use when switching xmake's output theme (colors, progress style, emoji) or fixing garbled/unreadable terminal output — `xmake g --theme=...`. Built-in themes: default, ninja, emoji, dark, light, plain, powershell. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/ops/xmake-theme) · ★24)
- **xmake-toolchains**: Use when switching compilers (gcc/clang/msvc/mingw), setting up cross-compilation for Android/iOS/embedded/wasm, or defining a custom toolchain. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/toolchains/xmake-toolchains) · ★24)
- **xmake-troubleshooting**: Use when xmake is misbehaving — hangs/stuck, mysterious errors, slow configure/build, regressions after upgrade — and you need to diagnose it. Covers `-vD` verbose traces, `XMAKE_PROFILE=stuck/trace/perf:*` profiling env var, git bisect via `xmake l cli.bisect`, and debugging the Lua + C core. Do NOT use for everyday build errors — use the relevant per-topic skill first. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/ops/xmake-troubleshooting) · ★24)
- **xmake-trybuild**: Use when you want to build a third-party project that uses CMake, Autotools, Meson, Ninja, Bazel, Make, MSBuild, Xcodebuild, or NdkBuild — through xmake, so you inherit xmake's cross-compilation and toolchain detection. Covers `xmake` auto-detection, `--trybuild=&lt;system&gt;`, `--tryconfigs="..."`, and cross-compile recipes for Android / iOS / MinGW / custom SDKs. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/cli/xmake-trybuild) · ★24)
- **xmake-unit-tests**: Use when running or adding **xmake's own** unit tests — working in the `xmake-io/xmake` source tree, invoking `xmake l tests/run.lua [testname]`, writing new test files under `tests/`, and integrating with `srcenv.profile`. Not for testing *your own* project — that's `xmake-tests`. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/testing/xmake-unit-tests) · ★24)
- **xmake-vala**: Use when building Vala projects with xmake — `.vala` sources with the `add_rules("vala")` rule, glib dependency, declaring used VAPI packages with `add_values("vala.packages", ...)`, and producing executables or shared/static libraries with exported headers/VAPIs. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/languages/xmake-vala) · ★24)
- **xmake-xpack**: Use when producing distributable packages from an xmake project — zip/tar.gz/deb/rpm/nsis/wix/srpm/pacman/runself/zst etc. — via `xpack(...)` in `xmake.lua` and the `xmake pack` CLI. Covers package metadata, format selection, install layout, components, custom install commands, and CLI flags. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/packaging/xmake-xpack) · ★24)
- **xmake-zig**: Use when building Zig projects with xmake — binary/library targets with `.zig` sources, cross-compilation (one of Zig's strengths), and mixing Zig with C. ([xmake-io/xmake-skills](https://github.com/xmake-io/xmake-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/languages/xmake-zig) · ★24)
- **xmake-zigcc**: Use when compiling C/C++ with `zig cc` / `zig c++` in Xmake, or cross-compiling to another platform with zig as the cross compiler — `--toolchain=zigcc`, the `-target` tuple xmake derives from `-p/-a`, and when `--cross=&lt;tuple&gt;` is required. - **xmath-usage**: Provides xmath API reference and in-place math optimization patterns for Defold. - **xmemo-audit-progress**: Audit implementation or project progress against the approved XMemo plan, last checkpoint, decisions, TODOs, and current workspace evidence. ([yonro/xmemo-cursor-plugin](https://github.com/yonro/xmemo-cursor-plugin/tree/HEAD/skills/xmemo-audit-progress))
- **xmemo-brainstorm**: Run a structured brainstorm grounded in XMemo goals, constraints, decisions, lessons, and failed approaches. - **xmemo-checkpoint**: Preserve a concise XMemo checkpoint after sustained material work, a completed phase, a blocker, an interruption, or a Cursor stop-hook reminder. - **xmemo-distill-session**: Turn an important Cursor conversation or work session into concise, retrieval-ready XMemo outcomes. ([yonro/xmemo-cursor-plugin](https://github.com/yonro/xmemo-cursor-plugin/tree/HEAD/skills/xmemo-distill-session))
- **xmemo-handoff-work**: Prepare or receive a professional XMemo handoff between Cursor sessions and compatible AI agents. ([yonro/xmemo-cursor-plugin](https://github.com/yonro/xmemo-cursor-plugin/tree/HEAD/skills/xmemo-handoff-work))
- **xmemo-memory**: Persistent, user-owned memory for agents. ([yonro/memory-os-cli](https://github.com/yonro/memory-os-cli/tree/HEAD/skills/xmemo) · ★12)
- **xmemo-memory-steward**: Coordinate XMemo memory across Cursor sessions and compatible AI agents. ([yonro/xmemo-cursor-plugin](https://github.com/yonro/xmemo-cursor-plugin/tree/HEAD/skills/xmemo-memory-steward))
- **xmemo-plan-project**: Design or revise evidence-gated PROJECT_PLAN.md and EXECUTION_PLAN.md artifacts using XMemo context and the live Cursor workspace. Use when starting a project, decomposing work into phases, assigning agent ownership, or correcting a plan after review. ([yonro/xmemo-cursor-plugin](https://github.com/yonro/xmemo-cursor-plugin/tree/HEAD/skills/xmemo-plan-project))
- **xmemo-resume-work**: Resume a project or unfinished task by recovering XMemo's latest verified checkpoint, relevant decisions, TODOs, blockers, and exact next action. Use at the start of a Cursor session, after interruption, or when continuing work previously handled by another agent. ([yonro/xmemo-cursor-plugin](https://github.com/yonro/xmemo-cursor-plugin/tree/HEAD/skills/xmemo-resume-work))
- **xmemo-review-plan**: Review an architecture, implementation, launch, or agent-produced plan against XMemo goals, settled decisions, constraints, lessons, project state, and acceptance criteria.
